Temuan penting dari perbandingan
Ada tiga pola organisasi:
- Organic social sebagai fungsi khusus: jelas terlihat pada WPP, Omnicom Creo, dan KINESSO.
- Social media sebagai pekerjaan end-to-end: terlihat di Publicis, Dentsu, dan beberapa posisi Havas. Strategi, konten, publishing, community, dan analisis bisa dikerjakan oleh satu role.
- Social sebagai gabungan paid dan organic: muncul pada sejumlah posisi WPP dan Publicis. Jadi title Social Media Manager saja tidak cukup untuk mengidentifikasi jenis pekerjaannya.
Contoh yang harus kita perhatikan: WPP Jakarta pernah mengiklankan Manager Social yang ternyata fokus pada paid social. Sebaliknya, WPP juga menggunakan Senior Social Media & Community Manager untuk pekerjaan organic. 

job-boards.greenhouse.io+1

Artinya, title harus selalu dibaca bersama job description.
2. Kompetensi inti Organic Social
Kompetensi	Aktivitas pekerjaan
Organic Social Strategy	Menyusun strategi pertumbuhan dan keberadaan brand di social platform
Content Strategy & Editorial Planning	Content pillars, editorial calendars, content themes dan platform-native ideas
Social Publishing & Operations	Scheduling, publishing, account management, dan content workflow
Community Management	Mengelola komentar, DM, engagement, moderation, dan escalation
Social Listening & Cultural Intelligence	Memantau percakapan, sentiment, tren, dan cultural moments
Social Content Performance	Mengukur reach, engagement, views, saves, shares, dan audience growth
Brand Voice & Reputation	Menjaga tone of voice, respons brand, dan reputasi digital
Client & Creative Collaboration	Bekerja dengan creative, production, influencer, account, dan media teams

Kedelapan kompetensi ini muncul dalam kombinasi yang berbeda di lowongan WPP, Omnicom, KINESSO, Dentsu, dan Publicis. 

job-boards.greenhouse.io+4

3. Empat orientasi pekerjaan di dalam Organic Social
A. Organic Social Strategy & Content
Core specialization
Fokus pada strategi kehadiran brand dan distribusi konten organik.
- Social media strategy.
- Content pillars dan editorial calendars.
- Platform-native content planning.
- Always-on social content.
- Social-first campaign ideation.
- Organic content performance.
B. Community Management
Operational specialization
Fokus pada hubungan dan interaksi brand dengan komunitas.
- Comment dan DM management.
- Community engagement.
- Moderation dan escalation.
- Response guidelines.
- Community growth.
- Reputation management.
C. Social Listening & Insights
Analytical specialization
Fokus pada percakapan dan perilaku audience.
- Social listening.
- Sentiment dan conversation analysis.
- Audience insight mining.
- Cultural trends.
- Competitive social analysis.
- Social intelligence reporting.
D. Social Publishing & Channel Operations
Operational specialization
Fokus pada pengelolaan aktivitas channel sehari-hari.
- Content scheduling.
- Publishing workflow.
- Content QA.
- Platform administration.
- Editorial calendar execution.
- Multi-account management.

Keempat orientasi ini tidak harus dijadikan empat role family. Di agency kecil, satu Social Media Executive bisa mengerjakan semuanya. Di agency besar, Community Management dan Social Listening dapat dipisahkan menjadi spesialisasi atau tim tersendiri.
4. Batas dengan role lainnya
Ini yang perlu kita kunci agar taxonomy tidak tumpang tindih.
Role	Fokus
Organic Social Media Specialist	Strategi dan aktivitas social media tanpa paid advertising
Paid Social Specialist	Aktivasi iklan berbayar di platform social
Social Media Strategist	Merancang social strategy; dapat mencakup organic dan paid tergantung JD
Community Manager	Interaksi, moderation, engagement dan pengembangan komunitas
Social Listening Analyst	Mengolah percakapan dan social data menjadi insight
Content Strategist	Strategi konten lintas kanal, bukan hanya social media
Influencer Marketing Specialist	Creator selection, partnership, briefing, dan campaign management

Organic Social bukan Paid Social. Organic Social tidak memiliki media buying sebagai tanggung jawab inti.

Begitu pula Influencer Marketing tidak boleh dilebur ke Organic Social. Kolaborasi dengan creator memang dapat menjadi bagian dari strategi konten, tetapi negosiasi talent, partnership, dan pengelolaan influencer campaign merupakan fungsi tersendiri.
5. Nomenklatur standar industri
Proposed standardized role
Organic Social Media Specialist
Role family: Organic Social & Community

Bertanggung jawab mengembangkan dan mengelola kehadiran brand di platform social secara organik melalui strategi, perencanaan konten, publishing, community engagement, social listening, serta analisis performa untuk membangun relevansi, hubungan dengan audience, dan pertumbuhan brand.

Title aliases berdasarkan job description:
- Social Media Executive
- Social Media Specialist
- Organic Social Specialist
- Social Media Strategist
- Social Strategist
- Social Media Manager
- Social Media & Community Manager
Specialist orientations:
1. Organic Social Strategy & Content.
2. Community Management.
3. Social Listening & Insights.
4. Social Publishing & Channel Operations.

Catatan: Social Media Manager atau Social Strategist hanya menjadi alias jika tanggung jawabnya sesuai dengan definisi Organic Social. Jangan otomatis memetakan semua lowongan dengan title tersebut ke jalur ini.

# SOURCE OF TRUTH ADDENDUM — ADVANCED ELECTIVES (4 NOVEMBER 2026)

Status: `Curriculum Architecture v1.0 — Proposed / Evidence Validation Pending`
Decision: Approved for inclusion as the current proposed architecture. This addendum supersedes earlier conflicting elective drafts in this file, including drafts that treated Affiliate as an elective or used a 10-chapter outline. It does not claim either curriculum is fully validated or implementation-ready.

## Taxonomy decision

The roadmap remains **16 core role tracks + 2 advanced electives**:

17. **CRO / Growth / Experience Optimization**
18. **Traditional Media Planning & Buying**

Affiliate & Social Commerce Partnerships and Community Management & Advocacy remain core tracks. The electives are optional advanced specializations, not mandatory competencies for every learner or every core role.

## Status and governance

| Aspect | Current status |
|---|---|
| Placement in 16 + 2 taxonomy | Confirmed |
| Elective names and scope | Confirmed at architecture level |
| Curriculum structure | 14 proposed chapters per elective |
| Senior / leadership competency map | Preliminary; evidence-by-domain validation pending |
| Agency role evidence | Preliminary cross-agency evidence reviewed; title equivalence is not assumed |
| Learning resources and platform documentation | Pending chapter-by-chapter validation |
| Certifications / credentials | Pending verification of availability, assessment, cost, validity and eligibility |
| Assessments, scoring rubrics and capstones | Proposed below; detailed rubrics pending |
| Website / repository implementation | Not implemented |
| Full validation | Not achieved; do not label `Fully Validated` or `Final Curriculum` |

### Evidence interpretation rules

- Analytics, measurement, product, UX or growth roles may provide adjacent evidence for CRO, but are not dedicated CRO-role evidence unless the job description explicitly owns conversion optimization or experimentation.
- Integrated media investment roles may provide evidence for traditional media responsibilities, but are not traditional-only evidence unless offline channels are explicitly in scope.
- Agency titles differ by market and organization. Compare accountabilities and decision rights, not title seniority alone.
- Separate job competencies from optional tools, vendor certifications, platform-specific skills and supplementary learning.
- Validate local measurement practices and data availability for the intended market. Do not transfer another country's ratings methodology or standards to Indonesia without evidence.

---

# ELECTIVE 17 — CRO / GROWTH / EXPERIENCE OPTIMIZATION

**Purpose:** Develop advanced capability to diagnose and improve conversion performance across websites, apps and measurable digital journeys through customer insight, analytics, experience design, experimentation and commercial accountability.

**Scope boundary:** CRO focuses on improving conversion and experience within a defined journey. Growth Marketing is broader and may include acquisition, activation, retention, referral and monetization. This elective is not a substitute for Paid Media, Product Management, CRM or general Growth Marketing.

## Proposed 14-chapter architecture

| # | Chapter | Competency focus | Required practical output |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Growth Economics & Conversion Strategy | Business model, unit economics, conversion definitions, funnel economics, KPI hierarchy and commercial constraints | Conversion business model and KPI tree |
| 2 | Customer Research & Insight | Qualitative and quantitative research, interviews, surveys, usability research, voice-of-customer, segmentation and evidence synthesis | Research plan, insight report and evidence log |
| 3 | Digital Analytics Architecture | Event taxonomy, GA4 or equivalent analytics, tag management, data layer, consent and data quality | Measurement specification and event taxonomy |
| 4 | Behavioral & Funnel Analytics | Funnel diagnostics, cohort and segment analysis, path analysis, friction identification and data limitations | Funnel diagnosis and prioritized opportunity map |
| 5 | UX & Digital Experience Optimization | Heuristic evaluation, usability, information architecture, forms, landing pages, accessibility and mobile experience | UX/CRO audit with annotated recommendations |
| 6 | Hypothesis & Opportunity Prioritization | Problem framing, hypothesis quality, expected impact, confidence, effort, risk and prioritization | Evidence-backed experiment backlog |
| 7 | Experimentation Design | A/B and multivariate test logic, control/treatment, randomization, metrics, guardrails, eligibility and experiment briefs | Experiment design document |
| 8 | Advanced Statistics for Experimentation | Sample size, power, minimum detectable effect, confidence intervals, practical significance, sequential peeking and multiple comparisons | Statistical analysis plan and sample-size calculation |
| 9 | Experimentation Technology & Technical QA | Experiment platforms, client/server-side implementation concepts, tracking validation, QA, performance risks and release controls | Implementation plan, QA checklist and test log |
| 10 | Causal Measurement & Incrementality | Incrementality, holdouts, geo experiments, causal inference concepts, attribution limitations and triangulation | Incrementality measurement plan and limitations memo |
| 11 | Personalization & Journey Orchestration | Segmentation, decision rules, personalization hypotheses, lifecycle context, privacy and measurement | Personalization strategy and decision matrix |
| 12 | Experimentation Programme Management | Experiment governance, intake, roadmap, prioritization cadence, documentation, reproducibility and stakeholder roles | Experimentation operating model |
| 13 | Commercial Impact & Executive Advisory | Revenue/profit impact, opportunity sizing, trade-offs, investment case, executive storytelling, client advisory and team leadership | Executive business case and recommendation deck |
| 14 | Senior CRO Capstone | Integrate research, measurement, experience diagnosis, experimentation, causal evaluation, governance and commercial impact | End-to-end CRO programme portfolio and executive presentation |

## Seniority expectations

- **Senior Specialist:** independently diagnoses conversion problems, validates data, designs experiments, interprets results responsibly and recommends prioritized changes.
- **Manager / Lead:** owns the experimentation roadmap, governance, cross-functional delivery, quality assurance, stakeholder alignment and measurable business outcomes.
- **Head / Director:** sets conversion strategy, operating model, investment priorities, measurement standards, capability development and executive accountability.

These are competency expectations, not universal agency title equivalences. Dedicated CRO roles and adjacent analytics / measurement leadership roles must be evidenced separately.

## Sub-specializations

1. **CRO Strategy & Experimentation:** research, opportunity sizing, experimentation programme, statistics and incrementality.
2. **UX & Conversion Experience Optimization:** usability, journey design, landing-page and form optimization, accessibility and experience quality.
3. **CRO Technical Implementation:** analytics instrumentation, experimentation platforms, data-layer coordination, implementation QA and technical governance.

## Proposed assessment architecture

- Chapter evidence checks: source interpretation, data quality and terminology.
- Practical assignments: research artefacts, measurement plan, funnel diagnosis, UX audit, experiment brief, sample-size exercise and QA log.
- Integrated capstone: an end-to-end CRO programme using an identified practice dataset or sandbox; simulated data must be labelled as simulated.
- Rubric dimensions to finalize: analytical validity, research quality, technical correctness, experiment design, statistical interpretation, prioritization, ethical/privacy awareness, communication and commercial reasoning.
- Passing thresholds, weighting, assessor guidance and resubmission rules remain pending formal design.

---

# ELECTIVE 18 — TRADITIONAL MEDIA PLANNING & BUYING

**Purpose:** Develop advanced capability to plan, evaluate, negotiate, buy, manage and measure offline media investment, including broadcast, print, out-of-home and relevant special partnerships, with integrated-media governance where appropriate.

**Scope boundary:** This elective covers traditional/offline media planning and buying. Digital paid media, programmatic trading and digital campaign operations remain separate core tracks. Cross-media integration supports investment decisions; it does not collapse distinct buying disciplines into one role.

## Proposed 14-chapter architecture

| # | Chapter | Competency focus | Required practical output |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | Traditional Media Ecosystem & Agency Operations | Media-owner landscape, agency/client roles, planning-to-buying workflow, briefs, approvals, finance and governance | Offline media ecosystem and workflow map |
| 2 | Audience Intelligence & Research | Audience definition, consumer research, media habits, target coverage, qualitative context and source evaluation | Audience intelligence report and source-quality assessment |
| 3 | Audience Measurement & Media Data | Ratings and reach currencies, panel/survey methodologies, universe estimates, data limitations, duplication and market-specific measurement | Measurement methodology comparison and limitations memo |
| 4 | Television & Broadcast Planning | TV inventory, dayparts, audience delivery, reach/frequency, rating points, GRP/TRP where applicable, spot allocation and scheduling | TV plan with assumptions and delivery estimates |
| 5 | Radio & Audio Investment | Station formats, audience fit, spot scheduling, reach/frequency, sponsorship, audio creative considerations and measurement constraints | Radio/audio plan and station evaluation |
| 6 | Print Media Planning & Buying | Publication profiles, circulation/readership evidence, formats, placement, frequency, negotiation and proof of insertion | Print recommendation and booking specification |
| 7 | OOH & DOOH Investment | Inventory types, location quality, visibility, audience/exposure methodology, traffic data, digital screen networks, proof-of-play and verification | OOH/DOOH plan with location rationale and measurement caveats |
| 8 | Cinema, Sponsorship & Special Partnerships | Cinema inventory, sponsorship rights, branded integrations, packages, valuation, exclusivity, deliverables and brand safety | Partnership proposal and rights/deliverables valuation |
| 9 | Media Mix & Investment Optimization | Objective-to-channel fit, reach/frequency trade-offs, budget allocation, scenario planning, marginal returns and cross-media coordination | Integrated offline media mix and budget scenarios |
| 10 | Commercial Negotiation & Trading | Rate cards, discounts, added value, make-goods, cancellation/payment terms, negotiation strategy and commercial stewardship | Negotiation simulation and deal evaluation sheet |
| 11 | Buying Operations & Campaign Execution | RFPs, quotations, insertion orders, booking confirmations, creative specifications, deadlines, handoffs and finance coordination | Buying documentation and campaign execution pack |
| 12 | Delivery Assurance & Post-Buy | Spot checks, affidavits/proof of performance, proof of posting/insertion, proof-of-play, under-delivery, reconciliation and make-good management | Post-buy report and reconciliation log |
| 13 | Cross-Media Measurement & Investment Governance | Campaign evaluation, cross-media reach caveats, brand/response measurement, audit trails, compliance, procurement and investment governance | Measurement and investment governance framework |
| 14 | Senior Investment Leadership Capstone | Integrated planning, commercial accountability, negotiation, delivery risk, measurement, client counsel and team leadership | Senior integrated investment plan and executive defense |

## Seniority expectations

- **Senior Specialist:** translates briefs into offline media plans, evaluates audience evidence, develops schedules, negotiates within approved parameters, manages bookings and checks delivery.
- **Manager / Group Head:** owns integrated plans and buying quality, budget stewardship, media-owner relationships, negotiation strategy, team delivery and client recommendations.
- **Director / Head:** sets investment strategy, commercial governance, partner policy, risk controls, organizational capability and executive client accountability.

Role evidence must distinguish traditional/offline-specific responsibilities from broader integrated-media investment leadership.

## Proposed assessment architecture

- Chapter evidence checks: media-currency interpretation, source reliability, planning assumptions and market-specific caveats.
- Practical assignments: audience evaluation, TV/radio/print/OOH plans, partnership valuation, budget scenarios, negotiation, booking pack and post-buy reconciliation.
- Integrated capstone: a multi-channel offline campaign plan with transparent assumptions, budget, buying rationale, delivery controls, measurement and post-buy evaluation.
- Rubric dimensions to finalize: audience fit, data literacy, planning logic, commercial value, negotiation quality, operational accuracy, delivery assurance, measurement integrity, governance and executive communication.
- Passing thresholds, weighting, assessor guidance and resubmission rules remain pending formal design.

---

## Validation backlog before either elective can be marked final

1. Build a competency-to-evidence matrix for every chapter, with source, date, agency, market, exact role title, responsibility evidence, evidence strength and direct/adjacent classification.
2. Validate against WPP Media, Publicis Media, Omnicom Media, Dentsu and Havas. Do not force equal evidence counts where public job descriptions are unavailable.
3. Add market-specific Indonesian sources for TV audience measurement, radio measurement, print readership and OOH/DOOH measurement. Label international standards and examples separately.
4. Verify every learning resource chapter by chapter: current URL, provider, free/paid access, language, prerequisites, practical relevance and last-checked date.
5. Verify credentials individually: issuer, exact credential name, assessment format, cost, validity, eligibility and whether it is a professional certification, course-completion certificate, skill badge or non-credential learning resource.
6. Write scored assessments and detailed rubrics, including assessor criteria and capstone evaluation.
7. Review chapter overlap with core tracks and document prerequisites. Do not duplicate full Paid Media, Media Analytics, CRM, SEO or Ad Operations curricula.
8. Conduct final editorial and evidence review before changing status to `Validated` or `Final Curriculum`.

ADDENDUM — ROLE COMPETENCY MATRIX & ADVANCED ELECTIVE CHAPTER AUDIT
Date: 4 November 2026
================================================================================

This addendum records the new audit documentation committed to the repository.
The original 16-track research and role audit above is preserved without rewriting
or deleting its historical notes. The new documents distinguish evidence,
competency requirements, chapter/subchapter learning outcomes, and open validation
items.

1. Ten agency × elective evidence groups
   WPP Media × CRO
   Publicis Media × CRO
   Omnicom Media × CRO
   Dentsu × CRO
   Havas Media Network × CRO
   WPP Media × Traditional Media
   Publicis Media × Traditional Media
   Omnicom Media × Traditional Media
   Dentsu × Traditional Media
   Havas Media Network × Traditional Media

2. Advanced elective competency matrices
   CRO: 12 domains, from business economics, customer research, analytics
   architecture, funnel analysis, UX, prioritization, experimentation, statistics,
   causal measurement, technical implementation, personalization, to commercial
   impact and leadership.

   Traditional Media: 14 domains, from agency operations and audience research
   through measurement, TV, radio, print, OOH/DOOH, cinema/sponsorship, media mix,
   negotiation, buying operations, post-buy, cross-media governance, and leadership.

3. Chapter/subchapter audit
   Each elective has a 14-chapter specification. Each chapter includes competency
   IDs, four subchapter learning areas, an applied build, a quality gate, and a
   documented gap/limitation. This is a detailed curriculum specification for
   implementation, not a claim that all content is already live in the website.

4. Sixteen-core-track crosswalk
   The companion crosswalk preserves the existing 16 tracks and defines each
   competency spine, work evidence and boundary with adjacent roles. It flags open
   decisions for Performance Marketing/Performance Strategist, Organic Social,
   Google Ads multi-channel specialization, direct buying vs Programmatic vs
   Traditional Media, and Media Analytics vs Marketing Science.

5. Evidence and release status
   Evidence classes distinguish direct role evidence, adjacent role evidence,
   agency capability evidence, third-party/historical listings, and standards or
   vendor documentation. Evidence strength is uneven across agencies and must not
   be presented as equal. In particular, Indonesia-specific traditional audience
   measurement and some direct senior CRO evidence remain open.

   Overall status: ADVANCED CURRICULUM SPECIFICATION — EVIDENCE VALIDATION PARTIAL.
   Do not label the electives Final Curriculum, Fully Validated, or Big Five Fully
   Validated until the outstanding evidence, resource, assessment and QA gates close.

CONSOLIDATED VALIDATION ADDENDUM v1.1 — CORE TRACKS, TAXONOMY & RELEASE GATES
Date: 4 November 2026
Status: CONSOLIDATED WORKING SOURCE OF TRUTH — VALIDATION IN PROGRESS
================================================================================

PURPOSE AND CHANGE CONTROL
--------------------------
This file preserves the entire preceding research record and appends this dated
validation addendum. The preceding material is historical source material and is
not silently rewritten. This addendum is the current decision layer. Where older
notes conflict with this addendum, the conflict is recorded and the dated decision
below controls the current curriculum taxonomy, unless a later version supersedes it.

This document is a working Source of Truth, not a claim that all 16 tracks, all
169 PR #5 chapter guides, all 28 elective chapters, all resource links, or all UI
states have passed final validation. Repository publication is a traceability
step; it does not itself prove content quality.

1. HOLDING-GROUP SCOPE AND EVIDENCE RULES
-----------------------------------------
The benchmark universe is the current five holding groups:
- WPP Media
- Publicis Media
- Omnicom Media
- Dentsu
- Havas Media Network

IPG/Mediabrands is not counted as a sixth independent holding group. Legacy
IPG/Mediabrands/KINESSO/UM evidence is retained under Omnicom where the relevant
network, date and employer identity are explicit. Do not relabel legacy evidence
as a current Omnicom job without qualification.

Evidence classes:
A = direct role evidence from an employer's own job description.
B = adjacent role evidence; supports only responsibilities explicitly shared.
C = official agency capability/service evidence; proves capability, not job scope.
D = employer job-board title/index evidence or third-party/historical listing
    without enough role detail to validate all competencies.
E = vendor/industry standard; supports methods, not role existence.
G = no sufficiently specific direct evidence located in this audit batch.

Geography, date, title, seniority and evidence type must travel with each claim.
A title appearing on a careers index is not equivalent to a complete job
description. A missing title is not proof that an agency does not perform the work.
Cross-agency similarity is not assumed.

2. CURRENT TAXONOMY — EXPLICIT DECISIONS
----------------------------------------
The 16 core tracks remain unchanged. The following decisions close the taxonomy
ambiguities for curriculum design. They do not claim every agency uses identical
job titles.

2.1 Full-Stack Digital Marketing & Performance vs Performance Marketing
Decision:
- Track 1 is the integrated generalist learning path: business objectives,
  audience/funnel, channel architecture, activation literacy, tracking,
  analytics, optimization, reporting and integrated capstone.
- Performance Marketing is a real cross-channel job family, not a synonym for
  Full-Stack. It owns measurable paid-growth outcomes across one or more paid
  channels, often including budget, CPA/CAC/ROAS, testing, conversion quality,
  forecast and commercial accountability.
- Performance Marketing Specialist/Manager/Strategist must be mapped by the
  actual JD scope. If it is cross-channel paid execution, map to Performance
  Marketing pathway within Track 1 plus the relevant channel tracks. Do not
  claim the learner is expert in every channel after the generalist curriculum.
- Paid Search, Paid Social and Programmatic remain separate specialist tracks.

2.2 Organic Social vs Content vs Community
Decision:
- Organic Social is a distinct market job family and must remain visible as a
  named specialization, not be collapsed into Community Management.
- To preserve the agreed 16-core-track architecture, Organic Social Strategy &
  Channel Operations is a dedicated specialization/pathway within Track 13
  (Content Strategy & Creation), with its own competency map, chapters,
  practical outputs and assessment. It is not a seventeenth core track in this
  taxonomy.
- Track 13 covers editorial/content strategy, content architecture, creative
  formats, publishing/distribution and content measurement. Its Organic Social
  specialization adds platform-native strategy, social calendars, channel
  publishing, organic analytics, social listening and social-first execution.
- Track 16 Community covers member relationships, participation, moderation,
  community service, advocacy, UGC governance and community health.
- Paid Social is paid auction media. Creator/KOL partnership management remains
  Track 14. Organic social does not own media buying, creator contracting or
  community moderation as its sole competency.
- Social listening may be a specialist capability under Organic Social or
  Analytics depending on whether the output is channel action or enterprise
  intelligence. Label the owner and interface.

2.3 Media Analytics vs Marketing Science
Decision:
- Keep one Track 10 with two explicit proficiency pathways.
- Media Analytics: KPI definitions, data quality, taxonomy, reporting,
  dashboarding, campaign diagnostics, pacing/performance narratives and
  decision support.
- Marketing Science: measurement design, experimentation, incrementality,
  causal inference, MMM, econometrics, forecasting, statistical validity,
  model governance and advanced analytical programming.
- SQL and visualization are core working tools for analytics. Python/R and
  causal/econometric methods are advanced pathway requirements where role scope
  demands them. Do not require econometrics from every reporting analyst.
- Attribution, MMM, lift studies and platform-reported conversions are not
  interchangeable evidence.

2.4 Paid Search vs Google Ads Multi-channel
Decision:
- Track 4 is the Paid Search/SEM specialist path: search intent, keyword/query
  architecture, match types, negatives, search terms, bidding, search ads/assets,
  landing-page relevance, conversion measurement and search optimization.
- Google Ads is a platform suite, not a single job family. YouTube, Display,
  Shopping, Demand Gen and app campaigns may require adjacent cross-channel
  knowledge or a separate platform specialization.
- SA360 is a search management platform; DV360 is a DSP. Do not teach DV360 as
  an extension of Paid Search or conflate Google Ads Display with open-web
  programmatic buying.
- A Google Ads Specialist title must be mapped from the actual campaign types
  and platform scope, not title alone.

2.5 Media Investment vs Programmatic vs Traditional Media Buying
Decision:
- Track 8: direct publisher/media-owner investment and commercial partnerships:
  RFP, inventory evaluation, rate negotiation, contracts/IOs, value-adds,
  delivery governance, reconciliation and partner relationships.
- Track 6: automated programmatic buying and trading: DSP/SSP, RTB, auction,
  inventory, deal IDs, audience/data activation, bidding, pacing, supply quality,
  brand safety, ad serving and programmatic measurement.
- Elective 18: offline/traditional planning and buying: TV, radio, print,
  OOH/DOOH, cinema, sponsorship, audience measurement, booking, proof of
  delivery, post-buy and market-specific currencies.
- A senior integrated investment role may span these areas, but the learning
  mechanics and operational controls remain separate. Do not treat one
  integrated job description as proof that all sub-disciplines are identical.

3. SIXTEEN CORE TRACK COMPETENCY MATRICES
-----------------------------------------
The following is the canonical minimum competency spine. Seniority is indicative;
actual job descriptions and market conventions override generic title assumptions.

TRACK 01 — FULL-STACK DIGITAL MARKETING & PERFORMANCE
Role aliases: Digital Marketing Specialist, Digital Performance Specialist,
Growth Marketing Specialist, Performance Marketing Specialist (scope-dependent).
Seniority: Executive/Specialist executes briefs and reporting; Manager owns
multi-channel plans, budget and optimization; Lead/Director owns growth strategy,
commercial outcomes, governance and team/client leadership.
Responsibilities: translate business goals into funnel and channel plans;
coordinate paid/owned/earned interfaces; define KPIs and tracking; monitor
performance; diagnose issues; run test-and-learn; report commercial outcomes.
Competencies: business acumen, audience/funnel, channel role selection, budget
allocation, campaign operations literacy, conversion tracking, analytics,
optimization, experimentation, client communication and integrated planning.
Tools: Google Ads, Meta Ads Manager, TikTok Ads Manager, GA4, GTM, Looker Studio,
Sheets/Excel, CRM/commerce analytics; platform tools depend on market/account.
Workflow: brief -> objective/KPI -> audience/funnel -> channel plan -> tracking
plan -> launch coordination -> QA -> monitor -> diagnose -> optimize -> report.
Deliverables: integrated media brief, channel/budget plan, KPI dictionary,
measurement/UTM plan, dashboard, optimization log and capstone business review.
Boundary: generalist literacy is not expert channel trading; specialist depth
belongs in Tracks 4–9, 11–15 as relevant.

TRACK 02 — CONNECTION / COMMUNICATIONS PLANNER
Aliases: Connection Planner, Communications Planner, Connections Strategist,
Media Strategist (scope-dependent).
Seniority: Executive supports research and plan materials; Manager develops
communication architecture and client recommendations; Director owns strategic
frameworks, senior counsel, team and new-business leadership.
Responsibilities: translate business/marketing problems into communication
problems; define audience and behavior change; build journey, messaging and
touchpoint roles; connect brand and performance; align channel specialists.
Competencies: research synthesis, audience insight, communication strategy,
journey mapping, messaging framework, channel orchestration, cultural/context
analysis, strategic rationale and presentation.
Tools: research/audience platforms, planning suites, social listening, analytics,
Excel/Slides, journey/diagram tools; exact stack is agency-specific.
Workflow: business diagnosis -> audience tension -> communication task ->
journey -> message architecture -> channel/touchpoint roles -> activation brief
-> learning agenda -> strategic evaluation.
Deliverables: communication brief, audience insight, journey map, message
framework, touchpoint architecture, channel role rationale and learning agenda.
Boundary: not the default owner of DSP execution, publisher negotiations or
platform campaign setup.

TRACK 03 — MEDIA PLANNER / DIGITAL PLANNER & STRATEGIST
Aliases: Media Planner, Digital Media Planner, Integrated Media Planner,
Senior Media Planner, Media Planning Manager.
Seniority: Executive builds research/plan materials; Manager owns channel mix,
budget and forecast; Director owns planning standards, client strategy and team.
Responsibilities: audience/media research, channel selection, reach/frequency
logic, budget allocation, phasing, scenario planning, competitive context and
client recommendation; coordinate activation and investment teams.
Competencies: audience analysis, media math, reach/frequency, channel economics,
forecasting, budget scenarios, media research, KPI mapping and presentation.
Tools: media research/currency sources by market, planning platforms, Excel,
PowerPoint, analytics and audience tools. Do not assume one global currency.
Workflow: brief -> audience -> market/media landscape -> channel roles -> budget
and forecast -> phasing -> scenario sensitivity -> plan presentation -> handoff
-> in-flight learning and post-campaign review.
Deliverables: media plan, investment scenario, reach/frequency assumptions,
forecast, phasing chart, competitive analysis and activation handoff.
Boundary: planning recommendation is not the same as media buying, DSP trading
or hands-on optimization.

TRACK 04 — PAID SEARCH / SEM SPECIALIST
Aliases: Paid Search Specialist, SEM Specialist, Search Executive/Manager,
Search Engine Marketing Specialist.
Seniority: Executive builds campaigns and search-term reports; Manager owns
account strategy, budget, optimization and junior quality; Director owns
portfolio/search capability and commercial outcomes.
Responsibilities: keyword and query architecture, account/campaign structure,
match types, negatives, bidding, ad copy/assets, landing-page alignment,
conversion measurement, search-term mining and budget pacing.
Competencies: search intent, keyword research, auction/bidding, quality/relevance,
conversion tracking, experiment design, feed/search campaign literacy,
policy/compliance and business economics.
Tools: Google Ads, Microsoft Advertising, SA360 where applicable, Keyword
Planner, GA4, GTM, Merchant Center when relevant, Excel/Looker Studio.
Workflow: objective -> query research -> structure -> keyword/negative mapping
-> creative/assets -> conversion QA -> launch -> search-term review -> bid/budget
optimization -> landing-page feedback -> reporting.
Deliverables: keyword map, account structure, negative list, RSA/assets,
conversion QA, search-term report, budget forecast and optimization memo.
Boundary: Google Ads multi-channel scope must be separately labelled; DV360 is
not Paid Search.

TRACK 05 — PAID SOCIAL / BIDDABLE SOCIAL SPECIALIST
Aliases: Paid Social Specialist, Social Performance Specialist, Biddable Social,
Social Activation Executive/Manager.
Seniority: Executive configures campaigns and reports; Manager owns account
optimization, creative testing and client/team workflow; Director leads channel
strategy, team and investment performance.
Responsibilities: paid social campaign setup, audience strategy, creative
testing, bidding/delivery, placements, pacing, conversion/brand measurement,
platform policy, experimentation and client reporting.
Competencies: auction mechanics, audience segmentation, creative strategy,
test design, platform measurement, pixel/event quality, privacy/policy,
incrementality literacy and performance analysis.
Tools: Meta Ads Manager, TikTok Ads Manager, LinkedIn Campaign Manager,
Pinterest/Snap where relevant, GA4, platform pixels/CAPI, analytics/dashboarding.
Workflow: brief -> objective -> audience -> creative matrix -> structure ->
tracking/QA -> launch -> pacing -> creative/audience tests -> analysis ->
recommendation.
Deliverables: campaign build sheet, audience matrix, creative testing plan,
tracking checklist, pacing report, test readout and optimization roadmap.
Boundary: paid delivery is distinct from organic social publishing, community
management and DSP supply/deal trading.

TRACK 06 — PROGRAMMATIC SPECIALIST / TRADER
Aliases: Programmatic Specialist, Trader, Programmatic Manager, DSP Specialist.
Seniority: Executive sets up and optimizes line items; Manager owns DSP strategy,
deals, pacing and team quality; Head/Director owns programmatic practice,
commercial performance and supply governance.
Responsibilities: DSP activation, audience/data targeting, inventory and deal
selection, bidding, pacing, frequency, brand safety, fraud/viewability,
measurement, publisher/platform collaboration and optimization.
Competencies: RTB, DSP/SSP ecosystem, auction mechanics, deal types, supply path,
identity/privacy, audience taxonomy, ad serving, brand safety, measurement and
troubleshooting.
Tools: DV360, The Trade Desk, other DSPs, CM360/ad servers, verification tools,
data platforms, spreadsheets, SQL/BI where needed. Tool choice is employer- and
market-specific.
Workflow: objective -> inventory/audience strategy -> DSP plan -> deal/supply
selection -> campaign build -> tag/creative QA -> pacing/bid optimization ->
quality controls -> measurement -> reconciliation/reporting.
Deliverables: DSP media plan, deal matrix, line-item setup, QA checklist,
pacing/optimization log, supply-quality report and post-campaign readout.
Boundary: automated DSP buying is distinct from direct publisher investment
(Track 8), Paid Search (Track 4) and offline buying (Elective 18).

TRACK 07 — RETAIL & COMMERCE MEDIA SPECIALIST
Aliases: Retail Media Specialist, Commerce Media Specialist, Retail Search,
Retail DSP, Commerce Activation.
Seniority: Executive manages sponsored listings and reporting; Manager owns
retailer plans, feeds, audience and budget; Director owns retailer partnerships,
commerce strategy and commercial growth.
Responsibilities: retailer/marketplace media planning, sponsored search/product,
onsite/offsite placements, product feeds, shopper audiences, retailer data,
closed-loop measurement, sales lift and commerce economics.
Competencies: retailer ecosystem, catalog/feed quality, product detail page,
shopper journey, search relevance, auction, retailer audience/data, incrementality,
ROAS/margin and partner operations.
Tools: Amazon Ads, Walmart Connect, marketplace/retailer ad platforms,
feed/catalog tools, commerce analytics, Excel/BI; regional retail media networks.
Workflow: retailer objective -> catalog/feed readiness -> shopper/audience
strategy -> onsite/offsite plan -> activation -> availability/price checks ->
optimization -> sales/measurement -> partner review.
Deliverables: retailer media plan, feed audit, sponsored product structure,
shopper audience plan, budget and margin model, closed-loop report.
Boundary: retail media is not generic e-commerce operations or affiliate
commission partnership (Track 15).

TRACK 08 — MEDIA INVESTMENT, TRADING & PUBLISHER PARTNERSHIPS
Aliases: Media Investment Executive/Manager, Investment Lead, Publisher
Partnerships, Direct Buying/Trading.
Seniority: Executive coordinates RFP, rates and delivery; Manager negotiates
inventory and owns partner/commercial governance; Director leads investment
strategy, client value, procurement and team.
Responsibilities: direct media-owner/publisher buying, RFPs, rate negotiations,
inventory, packages, contracts/IOs, value-adds, makegoods, billing, delivery
and reconciliation.
Competencies: commercial math, negotiation, inventory valuation, deal terms,
financial control, vendor relationship, transparency, delivery assurance and
market knowledge.
Tools: publisher proposals, rate cards, IO/contract systems, finance/procurement
tools, Excel, campaign delivery and reconciliation reports.
Workflow: brief -> RFP -> inventory evaluation -> rate/value comparison ->
negotiation -> approval/IO -> booking -> delivery monitoring -> reconciliation
-> post-buy and supplier evaluation.
Deliverables: RFP comparison, negotiation log, investment recommendation,
IO/contract pack, value-add tracker, delivery reconciliation and post-buy.
Boundary: direct publisher buying is not DSP programmatic trading and not
traditional broadcast planning by default.

TRACK 09 — AD OPERATIONS / DIGITAL CAMPAIGN OPERATIONS
Aliases: Ad Operations Specialist, AdOps Executive, Campaign Operations,
Media Implementation/Trafficking.
Seniority: Coordinator/Executive traffics and QA; Manager owns workflows,
discrepancy and operational controls; Director leads operations, governance,
resourcing and incident management.
Responsibilities: campaign trafficking, ad-server setup, tags/pixels, tracking
QA, naming/taxonomy, creative specs, launch checks, discrepancy, pacing,
billing, troubleshooting and documentation.
Competencies: ad serving, tag logic, HTML/JS basics, URL parameters, Floodlight/
conversion tags, data integrity, QA, privacy/consent, incident control and
process governance.
Tools: CM360/ad servers, platform pixels, GTM, QA/debugging tools, spreadsheets,
ticketing/project systems and analytics.
Workflow: booking/brief -> specs -> tags/assets -> trafficking -> QA -> launch
approval -> monitoring -> discrepancy triage -> change control -> reconciliation.
Deliverables: trafficking sheet, tag map, QA evidence, launch checklist,
discrepancy log, incident report and operational SOP.
Boundary: not media strategy, buying negotiation or analytics interpretation
although it supplies the technical data those functions rely on.

TRACK 10 — MEDIA ANALYTICS / MARKETING SCIENCE
Aliases: Media Analyst, Marketing Analyst, Measurement Analyst, Marketing
Science Consultant, Data/Measurement Scientist (scope-dependent).
Seniority: Analyst validates and reports; Manager owns measurement plans,
diagnosis and stakeholders; Director/Science Lead owns methodology, models,
governance, team and executive counsel.
Responsibilities: KPI/data definitions, data QA, taxonomy, dashboards, campaign
diagnosis, measurement design, experimentation, incrementality, modelling and
decision support.
Competencies: statistics, SQL, data modelling, visualization, media measurement,
experimental design, causal inference, MMM/econometrics at advanced pathway,
privacy and communication.
Tools: SQL, BigQuery/warehouse, GA4, ad platform exports, Looker Studio/Power BI,
Python/R for advanced work, spreadsheets and experimentation tools.
Workflow: business question -> metric specification -> source/data QA ->
analysis/diagnosis -> method selection -> test/model -> interpretation ->
limitations -> decision recommendation -> governance.
Deliverables: KPI dictionary, data-quality report, dashboard, performance
narrative, measurement plan, experiment readout, model documentation and
executive recommendation.
Boundary: campaign reporting does not equal causal proof; Marketing Science
requires additional methodological rigor beyond descriptive analytics.

TRACK 11 — SEO / ORGANIC SEARCH / AEO / GEO
Aliases: SEO Specialist, Technical SEO, SEO Analyst/Manager, Organic Search,
Search Experience/AEO/GEO (where employer defines it).
Seniority: Executive audits and implements; Manager owns technical/content/
authority roadmap and reporting; Director owns search strategy, team and
cross-functional prioritization.
Responsibilities: crawling/indexing, technical health, information architecture,
keyword/entity intent, content relevance, internal links, authority, structured
data, search performance and AI-search visibility research.
Competencies: technical SEO, search intent, content systems, schema, analytics,
log/crawl analysis, digital PR/link quality, SERP research and experimentation.
Tools: Search Console, GA4, crawling tools, keyword suites, log analysis,
structured-data validators, CMS and BI.
Workflow: discovery -> technical/content audit -> prioritize by impact/effort ->
roadmap -> implementation with dev/content -> QA -> monitor -> learn/report.
Deliverables: technical audit, keyword/topic map, content brief, schema spec,
internal linking plan, implementation tickets and organic performance report.
Boundary: AEO/GEO is not a guaranteed ranking/citation discipline and does not
replace foundational SEO.

TRACK 12 — CRM / LIFECYCLE / MARKETING AUTOMATION
Aliases: CRM Specialist, Lifecycle Marketer, Marketing Automation Specialist,
Retention/Customer Engagement Manager.
Seniority: Executive builds segments/campaigns; Manager owns lifecycle journeys,
deliverability and retention KPIs; Director owns customer strategy, martech
governance and cross-functional revenue/retention outcomes.
Responsibilities: customer data/consent, segmentation, lifecycle strategy,
triggered journeys, messaging, channel orchestration, deliverability, testing,
retention and revenue measurement.
Competencies: customer lifecycle, segmentation, event/data schema, journey
design, experimentation, email/push/SMS, deliverability, consent/privacy,
attribution and retention economics.
Tools: CRM/CDP/ESP platforms (e.g. Salesforce, Braze, HubSpot, Klaviyo as
market-appropriate), SQL/BI, analytics, consent and deliverability tools.
Workflow: data/consent audit -> lifecycle diagnosis -> segment -> journey ->
content/offer -> trigger/orchestration -> QA -> send -> experiment -> retention
analysis -> iteration.
Deliverables: lifecycle map, segment definitions, event taxonomy, journey
build, suppression/consent rules, test readout and retention dashboard.
Boundary: CRM owns lifecycle communication and customer orchestration; CRO
elective focuses on experience optimization and experiments at conversion
interfaces. Integration points must be taught, not merged.

TRACK 13 — CONTENT STRATEGY & CREATION (INCLUDING ORGANIC SOCIAL SPECIALIZATION)
Aliases: Content Strategist, Content Planner, Content Marketing Specialist,
Editorial Strategist, Content Creator (production-only scope must be separated),
Organic Social Media Specialist (specialization alias only in this curriculum).
Seniority: Executive produces/publishes and reports; Manager owns editorial
system, content planning and workflow; Director owns content strategy,
governance, brand voice and capability.
Responsibilities: audience/content needs, editorial strategy, content
architecture, messaging, formats, brand voice, briefs, production workflow,
distribution, publishing, measurement and governance. Organic Social pathway
adds platform-native content, social calendar, channel operations, organic
analytics and social listening.
Competencies: audience insight, narrative, editorial planning, copy/creative
briefing, content operations, platform formats, distribution, accessibility,
rights, analytics and brand governance.
Tools: CMS, editorial/project tools, native social platforms, publishing/
management suites, social analytics/listening, design/video tools and BI.
Workflow: objective/audience -> content role -> pillars -> editorial calendar ->
brief -> production -> approval/rights -> publishing/distribution -> measurement
-> iteration.
Deliverables: content strategy, pillar map, editorial calendar, briefs, asset
specs, publishing workflow, content performance report. Organic Social outputs:
channel audit, social strategy, platform-native calendar, response/escalation
matrix, organic dashboard and social listening report.
Boundary: Creator contracting is Track 14; community health/moderation is Track
16; paid distribution is Track 5.

TRACK 14 — KOL / CREATOR / INFLUENCER PARTNERSHIPS
Aliases: Influencer Marketing Specialist, Creator Partnerships, KOL/KOC Manager,
Talent/Creator Campaign Manager.
Seniority: Executive researches and coordinates creators; Manager owns brief,
contract, activation and reporting; Director owns partnership strategy, rights,
commercial model, risk and team/client leadership.
Responsibilities: creator discovery, audience/fraud checks, fit, briefing,
negotiation, contracts, usage rights, disclosure, brand safety, production
coordination, amplification and measurement.
Competencies: creator evaluation, audience quality, negotiation, legal/rights,
disclosure, content review, campaign operations, measurement and relationship.
Tools: creator discovery/measurement platforms, social platforms, contract/
workflow tools, spreadsheets and analytics.
Workflow: objective -> creator criteria -> shortlist/due diligence -> brief ->
commercial/rights -> production/approval -> publish/amplify -> measure ->
settlement and learning.
Deliverables: creator scorecard, shortlist, brief, contract/rights matrix,
content approval log, disclosure checklist, campaign report and case study.
Boundary: creator partnership is not editorial content ownership or affiliate
commission infrastructure, though campaigns may integrate them.

TRACK 15 — AFFILIATE & SOCIAL COMMERCE PARTNERSHIPS
Aliases: Affiliate Executive/Manager, Partner Marketing, Publisher/Affiliate
Manager, Social Commerce Partnerships (scope-dependent).
Seniority: Executive onboards/monitors partners; Manager owns commission,
tracking, partner growth and fraud; Director owns programme economics,
partnership strategy and commercial governance.
Responsibilities: affiliate model, partner recruitment, commission economics,
tracking/attribution, fraud controls, payouts, promotions, platform commerce
and compliance.
Competencies: unit economics, partner segmentation, attribution windows,
tracking, coupon/deal governance, fraud, reconciliation, contracts, platform
commerce and incremental contribution.
Tools: affiliate networks/platforms, tracking/attribution tools, product feeds,
commerce platforms, BI/SQL and finance reconciliation.
Workflow: objective -> programme design -> partner criteria -> contract/
commission -> tracking QA -> activation -> monitoring/fraud -> optimization ->
payout/reconciliation -> incrementality review.
Deliverables: programme business case, partner scorecard, commission matrix,
tracking test, fraud log, payout reconciliation and incremental performance
report.
Boundary: affiliate is paid-for-outcome partnership, not synonymous with
influencer/KOL, retail media or generic social selling.

TRACK 16 — COMMUNITY MANAGEMENT & ADVOCACY
Aliases: Community Manager, Community Specialist, Community & Advocacy Lead,
Community Engagement Manager.
Seniority: Coordinator responds/moderates; Manager owns community programme,
escalation and health; Head/Director owns community strategy, governance,
service and advocacy.
Responsibilities: community purpose, member journey, engagement, moderation,
service/escalation, UGC governance, advocacy, sentiment/health and impact.
Competencies: facilitation, moderation, conflict/crisis, community design,
member research, service recovery, UGC rights, safety, measurement and policy.
Tools: native community platforms, social management/listening, CRM/helpdesk,
moderation tools, analytics and knowledge base.
Workflow: purpose/governance -> member segmentation -> onboarding -> rituals/
programming -> moderation/escalation -> feedback/service -> advocacy ->
community health measurement -> improvement.
Deliverables: community charter, member journey, moderation policy, response
bank, escalation playbook, UGC permission log, engagement/health dashboard.
Boundary: Community is relationship/participation-led. Organic Social is
publishing/distribution-led; both collaborate but are not interchangeable.

6. PR #5 CHAPTER-GUIDE AUDIT — IMPLEMENTATION REGISTER AND LIMITS
------------------------------------------------------------------
Known PR #5 branch: audit/align-all-16-track-learning-modules.
The guide code is split across:
- role-study-guides-part1.js
- role-study-guides-part2.js
- role-study-guides-part3.js
The renderer in index.html chooses a guide by role array position
(ROLE_STUDY_GUIDES[role][index]) and falls back to role-specific/default content.

Confirmed code-level issues to carry as release blockers:
1. Guide-to-chapter association is positional, not based on explicit immutable
   chapter IDs. Reordering a chapter can silently point to the wrong guide.
2. Guide constructors do not consistently carry explicit competency IDs,
   evidence/source IDs, resource IDs, rubric IDs and assessment metadata.
3. The presence of concept/example/steps/practice/checks/glossary fields does
   not prove that each question has a validated answer key or that a rubric has
   a measurable passing threshold.
4. The existing question-and-answer content is static guide content. Do not
   describe it as a fully implemented interactive quiz engine without testing
   the actual UI and answer-scoring behavior.
5. A 169-guide count does not validate role relevance, technical depth,
   correctness, resource availability or responsive behavior.

Required evidence-register schema for every rendered chapter:
chapter_id | track_id | chapter_title | guide_id | competency_ids |
agency_evidence_ids | learning_objectives | technical_depth |
tools_methods_workflow | practice_scenario | deliverable |
assessment_type | answer_key_or_rubric | passing_criteria |
resource_ids | source_urls | gaps | reviewer_status

Unique ID convention to implement:
DPA-T01-CH01 through DPA-T16-CHnn.
Specialization IDs use DPA-T13-OS-CH01 etc. Elective IDs use
DPA-E17-CH01 through DPA-E18-CH14.
Do not infer current chapter titles from array positions; export the rendered
chapter data and bind by explicit IDs.

Audit status:
- Prior repository inspection counted 169 total guides: 149 added across 14
  specialist tracks plus 10 retained Full-Stack and 10 retained Paid Social.
- Prior inspection verified guide source pattern (concept, simulated example,
  workflow, practice/deliverable, comprehension Q&A, glossary).
- This is a source/count and structure audit only. The complete 169-row
  competency/source/resource/practice/quiz/rubric register has NOT been
  completed in this addendum and remains a release blocker.
- The code count must be re-run against the exact current PR head before merge.
- Each track must be checked against its rendered chapter titles and guide
  ordering, including role-specific visibility and fallback behavior.

8. RESOURCE, CREDENTIAL, ASSESSMENT AND PORTFOLIO VALIDATION
-----------------------------------------------------------
For every resource, record:
resource_id | platform/issuer | exact URL | learning outcome | language |
geographic access | price/cost | prerequisites | duration | assessment format |
credential type | issue criteria | expiry/renewal | last checked | status.

Credential categories must remain separate:
- Learning content only (no credential)
- Course completion certificate
- Platform skilling certificate / achievement badge
- Professional certification (exam/proctored/experience-gated if applicable)
- Award/competition recognition

Do not label a course completion badge as a professional certification. Do not
make paid, experience-gated or region-restricted exams mandatory for beginners.
A working URL is not proof of current free access. Verify cost and eligibility
on the issuer's own page. Portfolio artifacts must be labelled simulated when
using synthetic data and must not imply client outcomes.

Assessment rubric standard:
0 = Not demonstrated / materially incorrect.
1 = Foundational / completes guided task with substantial support.
2 = Independent practitioner / accurate, validated work and defensible decisions.
3 = Advanced / handles ambiguity, trade-offs, governance, stakeholder counsel
    and business impact.
Each chapter must specify observable criteria, passing threshold, answer key
where applicable, allowed assumptions/data, reviewer guidance and resubmission
rule. A generic multiple-choice quiz alone is insufficient for a specialist
competency.

- Export rendered chapter IDs and reconcile exactly to all chapter guides.
- Complete 169-row evidence register with competency/source/resource/practice/

- Verify every answer key; static Q&A must not be represented as scored quiz.
- Verify all links and resource access/cost/credential details.
- Test all 16 role selector states, progress persistence, chapter visibility,
  keyboard navigation, focus, responsive breakpoints, print and theme behavior.
- Confirm no cross-role fallback content is misleading or duplicated.
- Run console/network smoke tests and check no broken navigation/regression.

PR #6:
- Verify the two elective source docs and 28 chapter structures against the
  consolidated Source of Truth.
- Verify all ten agency evidence groups and evidence classes/URLs.
- Close or explicitly retain CRO and Traditional Media evidence gaps.
- Verify resource/credential/assessment/rubric and Indonesia-market gates.
- Confirm links between PR #5 and PR #6 docs resolve on target branches.
- Do not merge solely because preview deployments report READY.

ADDENDUM v1.3 — RESOURCE AND CREDENTIAL VALIDATION REGISTER
Date: 4 November 2026
================================================================================

Issuer-source findings and credential distinctions are maintained in:
docs/resource-credential-validation.md

Verified in this research batch:
- Google Ads certification: 80% pass threshold, 75-minute assessment, one-year
  validity, 22 available languages including Indonesian.
- Meta Blueprint: free learning content is distinct from paid Meta Certification.
  Exam voucher pricing varies by marketplace; US pricing must not be copied to
  Indonesia.
- TikTok Media Buying Professional: 60 questions, 110 minutes, proctored,
  USD 200 plus applicable fees/taxes, designed for candidates with at least one
  year hands-on TikTok Ads Manager experience; two-year validity stated on the
  certification page.
- Amazon Ads Programmatic Solutions: approximately two-hour certification path;
  badge and downloadable certificate after passing. The accessible source
  includes an expiration date on the PDF but did not establish the exact
  validity period or exam price in this research batch.
- StackAdapt Programmatic Masterclass: free, eight modules, 120 minutes; treat
  as a vendor-issued course certification, not independent industry accreditation.
- The Trade Desk Programmatic 101: one-hour foundational course; no credential
  confirmed on the public course page reviewed.

These findings are source-batch facts, not proof of Indonesian account access.
The exact enrollment price, taxes, regional eligibility and expiration must be
rechecked at the point of enrollment. Do not make paid/experience-gated
certifications mandatory for beginners.

ADDENDUM v1.4 — IMMUTABLE CHAPTER/GUIDE ID MAPPING IMPLEMENTED
Date: 4 November 2026
================================================================================

The positional chapter-guide mapping blocker identified in v1.1/v1.2 has now
been addressed in the PR #5 branch:
- index.html assigns a unique data-chapter-id to all 169 rendered core chapters.
- fullstack-guides.js and paid-social-guides.js assign explicit chapterId fields
  to the 20 retained Full-Stack/Paid Social guide objects.
- role-study-guides-part1.js, part2.js and part3.js pass explicit chapter IDs
  into the G() guide constructor for all 149 specialist guide objects.
- chapter-guide-map.js builds a registry keyed by chapterId and rejects
  duplicate IDs.
- buildGuide() resolves a guide by chapter.dataset.chapterId, not by array
  position. Missing guide mapping uses generic chapter guidance rather than
  silently displaying another chapter's guide.

Static reconciliation against the current PR #5 branch:
- HTML chapter IDs: 169
- Unique HTML chapter IDs: 169
- Guide IDs: 169
- Unique guide IDs: 169
- Missing guide IDs: 0
- Orphan guide IDs: 0
- Duplicate guide IDs: 0
- Registry script loaded after the guide source scripts: yes
- Legacy positional lookup in buildGuide(): removed

This closes the source-level positional mapping issue. It does NOT certify
browser/runtime behavior, content quality, source accuracy, chapter resources,
answer-key correctness, scoring, rubrics or responsive UI. Those remain
separate QA gates. The chapter evidence register is:
docs/169-chapter-evidence-register.md

- 169 core chapter elements found; 169 unique data-chapter-id values.
- 391 HTML id attributes found; all 391 are unique.
- 13 internal anchor links found; zero missing target IDs.
- Eight local script dependencies are declared: fullstack-guides.js,
  paid-social-guides.js, role-study-guides-part1.js, role-study-guides-part2.js,
  role-study-guides-part3.js, chapter-guide-map.js,
  fullstack-master-roadmap.js and fullstack-master-renderer.js.
- Local source files are present in the PR #5 branch.
- Static source contains localStorage progress persistence, theme switching,
  checkbox persistence, keyboard focus handling and prefers-reduced-motion
  handling.
- Responsive CSS breakpoints are present, including max-width 640px, 760px,
  900px, 980px, 1101px and 380px rules plus print styles.
- The word "quiz" appears only three times in the inspected index source and
  no quiz scoring/submission engine was detected by the static check. Current
  guide comprehension Q&A must not be described as an interactive scored quiz.
- Vercel status checks on the inspected PR branch commits returned success.
  This is deployment status only; no GitHub Actions test workflow runs were
  returned for those commits.

Not performed / still required:
- No real browser session or device matrix was run in this static audit.
- No visual screenshot comparison at mobile/tablet/desktop breakpoints.
- No live click-through test of all 16 role selector states, progress save/
  restore, theme toggle, print, keyboard-only navigation or screen reader.
- No console/network trace, runtime exception check, external link crawl or
  resource enrollment test.
- No chapter-by-chapter answer-key correctness review or formal rubric scoring.
- No test with empty/slow network, blocked CDN/font or unavailable third-party
  platform.

Therefore:
- Source-level structural checks: PASS for unique IDs, internal anchors and
  declared script references.
- Deployment check: Vercel status success for the inspected commits.
- Browser/runtime QA: NOT RUN.
- Content/resource/assessment validation: PARTIAL.
- Overall PR #5 / PR #6 release decision: NOT READY TO MERGE until all remaining
  gates are closed and a full browser QA report is attached.

  broader Havas Group index lists Content Creator and CRM Director. These are
  title-level index evidence unless a full JD is separately linked.
  https://jobs.jobvite.com/havasmedia
  https://jobs.jobvite.com/thehavasgroup/

IMPLICATIONS FOR THE 16-TRACK MATRIX

- Track 12 CRM now has direct evidence from Publicis (strategy), Dentsu/Merkle
  (campaign operations) and Havas (senior integrated CRM leadership). These
  are different seniority/function layers and must not be merged into one
  generic CRM job.
- Track 13 Content/Organic Social now has direct evidence from WPP, Publicis,
  Omnicom and Dentsu. Organic Social is a real role family with strategy,
  platform-native publishing, editorial calendar, social listening and
  performance reporting. It remains a named specialization inside Track 13
  to preserve the 16-track count; Track 16 remains community relationship,
  moderation, service and advocacy.
- Track 14 KOL/Creator has WPP content planning and Publicis Social/Community
  role evidence; Dentsu has adjacent influencer campaign account leadership.
  Dedicated specialist-title evidence remains uneven.
- Track 15 Affiliate has Dentsu integrated eCommerce/affiliate execution and
  Publicis UK affiliate title evidence. Dedicated affiliate-programme
  management evidence across all five groups remains incomplete.
- Track 7 Retail/Commerce has WPP and Omnicom title/role evidence, Dentsu
  Jakarta integrated eCommerce/retail media execution and Havas retail DSP
  director evidence. Publicis evidence is title-level in this batch.
- Track 11 SEO has direct Omnicom Senior Analyst SEO evidence and WPP title
  evidence; other agency coverage still requires full JDs.
- Track 16 Community has direct Publicis and Omnicom role evidence, plus
  Dentsu/WPP adjacent social roles. Community should not absorb all Organic
  Social publishing responsibilities.
- Track 9 Ad Operations remains weak on full JDs in this batch. WPP and
  Omnicom career indexes identify AdOps role families, but chapter-level
  technical competencies still need direct JD validation.

EVIDENCE LIMITATIONS
- A job title or careers index is not a complete JD. Mark D when only the title
  is visible.
- Employer-hosted pages, LinkedIn-hosted employer-attributed listings and
  third-party syndication are different evidence classes.
- Expired postings remain historical evidence; never describe them as active.
- Geography and seniority are mandatory fields. Jakarta role evidence is more
  transferable to Indonesia curriculum than US/Europe examples, but still
  requires local workflow, legal, platform and vendor verification.
- The 5 × 16 matrix is now stronger for selected functions but is still not a
  complete set of 80 full job-description validations. Overall evidence status
  remains PARTIAL.

ADDENDUM v1.8 — FULL GUIDE CONTENT EXTRACTION FOR 169 CHAPTERS
Date: 4 November 2026
================================================================================

The 169-chapter evidence register has been expanded from an inventory table
into a chapter-by-chapter guide-content extraction:
docs/169-chapter-evidence-register.md

It now contains:
- 169 unique chapter IDs, reconciled to 169 matching guide objects.
- Track/role competency ID mapping for every chapter (initial crosswalk).
- Five-agency role-level evidence row attached to each chapter, with explicit
  note that this is role-level, not proof for each subtopic.
- Guide concept/learning focus, simulated example, workflow/technical steps,
  practice/deliverable and glossary terms.
- 507 static comprehension Q&A items, including the current guide answer text,
  extracted for SME correctness review.
- Chapter-level resource links where present; where none exist, a resource
  mapping gap is explicitly recorded.
- Explicit assessment/rubric gaps for every chapter.

This makes all 169 chapters individually inspectable and gives reviewers the
current content and answer text to validate. It does NOT mean all 169 are
content-validated. Remaining per-chapter gates:
1. Verify learning objective is observable and not merely a paraphrase of title.
2. Verify each technical step, named tool and method against authoritative
   current documentation.
3. Verify the simulated case and practice deliverable are realistic, complete
   and appropriate to the role/seniority.
4. Review all 507 question/answer pairs for correctness, ambiguity and
   coverage; do not assume extracted answer text is correct.
5. Map official resource URLs to chapter competency and verify access, language,
   cost, prerequisites, credential type and expiry.
6. Author role-specific rubric criteria, pass thresholds, assessor guidance
   and resubmission rules.
7. Run full browser/runtime QA and regression tests.

The 169-chapter audit status is now: INVENTORY + GUIDE CONTENT + STATIC Q&A
EXTRACTION COMPLETE; SME/TECHNICAL/RESOURCE/ASSESSMENT VALIDATION PARTIAL.

END OF ADDENDUM v1.8
